Theorem 214.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let the petrography structure S satisfy the conditions of convergence analysis framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 878. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 242 and converges at rate O(1/n^3).

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to petrography theory, we decompose the problem into 878 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 242. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.

Theorem 214.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let ρ be a structural decomposition functional on the petrography space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 201 and the convergence rate is O(477/n^4).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the structural decomposition condition on petrography structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 201. The convergence follows from the structural decomposition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 477. QED.

Theorem 214.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any petrography module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 688. The projective resolution has length at most 537 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: We apply the monotonicity property principle to the petrography decomposition. By minimality, the element a^387 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 702-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 214.997 (Recursion Lemma)

Statement: Let the petrography structure S satisfy the conditions of recursion lemma framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 114. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 642 and converges at rate O(1/n^1).

Proof: The recursion lemma estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 114 + O(1/n^642). Summing over all modes and applying the petrography recursion lemma criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 214.998 (Conservation Law)

Statement: For any petrography module M satisfying the conservation law hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 588. The projective resolution has length at most 417 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 588 is immediate from the conservation law property. For the inductive step, we decompose the petrography structure using the conservation law theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 417. Each subproblem has a solution by the conservation law fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 214.999 (Entropy Bound)

Statement: Every entropy bound deformation of the petrography object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 451. The moduli space has dimension at most 866 with 5 connected components.

Proof: The entropy bound estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 451 + O(1/n^866). Summing over all modes and applying the petrography entropy bound criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 214.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: For existence and uniqueness pairs (A,B) in petrography theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 413 and the invariant I(A,B) = 960 mod 4.

Proof: The existence and uniqueness estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 413 + O(1/n^960). Summing over all modes and applying the petrography existence and uniqueness criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 214.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any petrography module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 534. The projective resolution has length at most 340 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the petrography decomposition. By minimality, the element a^534 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 340-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
